Figure 15:
The picture, to be read in boustrophedon order, shows the braids corresponding to the passage from one band
\mathcal{H}_{k}
to
\mathcal{H}_{k+1}
. Starting from the configuration of the canonical coordinates at
0\in QH^{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})
, we slightly split the coalescence as described in the first red picture in the first line. The numbers represent the lexicographical order of the canonical coordinates w.r.t. the admissible line. Letting the admissible line
\ell
continuously rotate by increasing its slope, we determine all elementary braids acting in the mutation up to the next red configuration (the counter-clockwise rotation of
\ell
is visualised by the clockwise rotation of the rays
L_{j}(\phi)
defined in (
3.2
)). By coalescence of the points
u_{3}
,
u_{4}
in a red picture we obtain a configuration of canonical coordinates in the locus of small quantum cohomology (i.e., in a strip
\mathcal{H}_{k}
). Thus we deduce that successive bands of the small quantum cohomology are related by alternate compositions of the braids
\omega_{1}:=\beta_{12}\beta_{56}
,
\omega_{2}:=\beta_{23}\beta_{45}\beta_{34}\beta_{23}\beta_{45}
and
\hat{\omega}_{1}:=\beta_{12}\beta_{34}\beta_{56}
.

7
A note on the topological solution for Fano manifolds

For quantum cohomologies of smooth projective varieties, a fundamental system of solutions of the equation for gradients of deformed flat coordinates

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\partial_{\alpha}\zeta=z\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}\zeta,\\
\partial_{z}\zeta=\left(\mathcal{U}+\dfrac{1}{z}\mu\right)\zeta,\end{cases}

(7.1)

can be expressed in
enumerative-topological
terms, namely the genus 0 correlations functions.

Proposition 7.1
.

For a sufficiently small
R>0
, it is defined an analytic function

\Theta\colon\ B_{\mathbb{C}}(0;R)\times\Omega\to\operatorname{End}(H^{\bullet}(X;\mathbb{C}))

with series expansion

\displaystyle\Theta(z,t):=\operatorname{Id}+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\mathopen{\hbox{${\bigg\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\bigg\langle}$}}\frac{z\cdot(-)}{1-z\psi},T_{\alpha}\mathclose{\hbox{${\bigg\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\bigg\rangle}$}}_{0}(t)T^{\alpha}=\operatorname{Id}+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n+1}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{n}(-),T_{\alpha}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}(t)T^{\alpha}.

This function
\Theta
satisfies the following properties:

1)

for any
\phi\in H^{\bullet}(X;\mathbb{C})
, the vector field

\displaystyle\Theta_{\phi}:=\Theta(z,t)\phi=\phi+\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\mathopen{\hbox{${\bigg\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\bigg\langle}$}}\frac{z\phi}{1-z\psi},T_{\alpha}\mathclose{\hbox{${\bigg\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\bigg\rangle}$}}_{0}(t)T^{\alpha}=\phi+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n+1}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{n}\phi,T_{\alpha}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}(t)T^{\alpha}

satisfies the equations

\partial_{\alpha}\Theta_{\phi}=z\partial_{\alpha}*\Theta_{\phi};

2)

when restricted to the small quantum locus
\Omega\cap H^{2}(X;\mathbb{C})
, i.e.,
t^{i}=0
for
i=0,r+1,\dots,N
, then

\Theta_{\phi}={\rm e}^{z\delta}\cup\phi+\sum_{\beta\neq 0}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}{\rm e}^{\int_{\beta}\delta}\bigg\langle\frac{z{\rm e}^{z\delta}\cup\phi}{1-z\psi},T_{\alpha}\bigg\rangle^{X}_{0,2,\beta}T^{\alpha},\qquad\delta:=\sum_{i=1}^{r}t^{i}T_{i}\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{C});

3)

for any
\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\in H^{\bullet}(X;\mathbb{C})
we have

\eta\left(\Theta(-z,t)\phi_{1},\Theta(z,t)\phi_{2}\right)=\eta\left(\phi_{1},\phi_{2}\right);

4)

for any
\phi\in H^{\bullet}(X;\mathbb{C})
, the vector field

\widetilde{\Theta}_{\phi}:=\big(\Theta(z,t)\circ z^{\mu}z^{c_{1}(X)\cup(-)}\big)\phi

is a solution of the system (
7.1
), i.e.,

\partial_{\alpha}\widetilde{\Theta}_{\phi}=z\partial_{\alpha}*\widetilde{\Theta}_{\phi},\qquad\partial_{z}\widetilde{\Theta}_{\phi}=\left(\mathcal{U}+\frac{1}{z}\mu\right)\widetilde{\Theta}_{\phi}.

Thus, the vector fields
\widetilde{\Theta}_{T_{\alpha}}
’s are gradients of deformed flat coordinates: if
\big(\Theta^{\alpha}_{\beta}\big)_{\alpha,\beta}
,
\big(\widetilde{\Theta}^{\alpha}_{\beta}\big)_{\alpha,\beta}
are the matrices representing the two
\operatorname{End}(H^{\bullet}(X;\mathbb{C}))
-valued functions
\Theta
and
\widetilde{\Theta}
w.r.t. the basis
(T_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
, i.e.,

\Theta(z,t)T_{\beta}=\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\Theta^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)T_{\alpha},\qquad\widetilde{\Theta}(z,t)T_{\beta}=\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}\widetilde{\Theta}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)T_{\alpha},

then there exist analytic functions
\big(\widetilde{t}_{\alpha}(z,t)\big)_{\alpha}
,
(h_{\alpha}(z,t))_{\alpha}
on
B_{\mathbb{C}}(0;R)\times\Omega
such that

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Theta}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)=\big(\operatorname{grad}\tilde{t}_{\beta}(z,t)\big)^{\alpha},\qquad\big(\tilde{t}_{0},\tilde{t}_{1},\dots,\tilde{t}_{N}\big)=(h_{0},h_{1},\dots,h_{N})\cdot z^{\mu}z^{R},

\displaystyle\Theta^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)=(\operatorname{grad}h_{\beta}(z,t))^{\alpha},\qquad\Theta^{\rm T}(-z,t)\eta\Theta(z,t)=\eta,

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}(z,t):=\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}h_{\alpha,p}(t)z^{p},\qquad h_{\alpha,0}(t)=t_{\alpha}\equiv t^{\lambda}\eta_{\lambda\alpha}.

Proof.

Notice that

Y(z,t):=H(z,t)z^{\mu}z^{R},\qquad H(z,t)=\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}H_{p}(t)z^{p},\qquad H_{0}(t)\equiv\mathbbm{1}

is a fundamental solution of (
7.1
) if and only if
H(z,t)
satisfies the system

\begin{cases}\partial_{\alpha}H=z\mathcal{C}_{\alpha}H,\\
\partial_{z}H=\mathcal{U}H+\dfrac{1}{z}[\mu,H]-HR.\end{cases}

Because of the symmetry of
c_{\alpha\beta\gamma}
, the columns of
H
are the components w.r.t.
(\partial_{\alpha})_{\alpha}
of the gradients of some functions:

\displaystyle h_{\alpha}(z,t):=\sum_{p=0}^{\infty}h_{\alpha,p}(t)z^{p},\qquad h_{\alpha,0}(t)=t_{\alpha},

\displaystyle H_{\beta}^{\alpha}(z,t)=(\operatorname{grad}h_{\beta})^{\alpha},\qquad H_{\beta,p}^{\alpha}(z,t)=(\operatorname{grad}h_{\beta,p})^{\alpha}.

The above system for
H
is equivalent to the following recursion relations on
h_{\alpha,p}
’s functions:

\displaystyle\partial_{\alpha}\partial_{\beta}h_{\gamma,p}(t)=c_{\alpha\beta}^{\nu}\partial_{\nu}h_{\gamma,p-1}(t),\qquad p\geq 1,

(7.2)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{E}(\operatorname{grad}h_{\alpha,p})=\left(p+\frac{\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X-2}{2}+\mu_{\alpha}\right)\operatorname{grad}h_{\alpha,p}+\sum_{\beta=0}^{N}(\operatorname{grad}h_{\beta,p-1})R^{\beta}_{\alpha},\qquad p\geq 1.

The last equation is equivalent to the recursion relations on the differentials

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{E}({\rm d}h_{\alpha,p})=\left(p-\frac{\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X-2}{2}+\mu_{\alpha}\right){\rm d}h_{\alpha,p}+\sum_{\beta=0}^{N}{\rm d}h_{\beta,p-1}R^{\beta}_{\alpha},\qquad p\geq 1.

(7.3)

In our case we have

H(z,t)=\big(\Theta^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)\big)_{\alpha,\beta},\qquad\partial_{\alpha}h_{\beta,p}(t)=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{p-1}T_{\beta},T_{\alpha}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}(t).

The recursion relations (
7.2
) then reads

\displaystyle\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta},T_{\gamma}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta},T^{\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}\eta_{\nu\gamma}\qquad\text{for }p=1,

\displaystyle\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}T_{\alpha},\tau_{p-1}T_{\gamma},T_{\beta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}=\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta},T^{\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{p-2}T_{\gamma},T_{\nu}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}\qquad\text{for }p\geq 2.

These are exactly the
topological recursion relations in genus
0
.

Let us now prove that also the recursion relations (
7.3
) hold. K. Hori
[
47
]
(see also
[
30
]
) proved that, for any
\omega\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{C})
, we have the following constraint on the genus
g
free energy

\displaystyle\omega^{\alpha}\frac{\partial\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g}}{\partial t^{\alpha,0}}=\sum_{\beta\in\operatorname{Eff}(X)}\left(\int_{\beta}\omega\right)\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g,\beta}+\sum_{n,\alpha,\atop\sigma,\nu}\omega^{\sigma}c_{\sigma\alpha}^{\nu}t^{\alpha,n}\frac{\partial\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g}}{\partial t^{\nu,n-1}}

\displaystyle\hphantom{\omega^\alpha\frac{\partial\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g}}{\partial t^{\alpha,0}}=}{}+\frac{\delta^{0}_{g}}{2}\sum_{\alpha,\nu,\sigma}\omega^{\sigma}c_{\sigma\alpha\nu}t^{\alpha,0}t^{\nu,0}-\frac{\delta^{1}_{g}}{24}\int_{X}\omega\cup c_{\dim X-1}(X),

(7.4)

where

\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g,\beta}
is the
(g,\beta)
-free energy

\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g,\beta}:=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{n!}\langle\underbrace{\gamma,\dots,\gamma}_{n\text{ times}}\rangle_{g,n,\beta}^{X}.

By dimensional consideration, one obtains also the selection rule

\displaystyle\sum_{n,\alpha}(n+q_{\alpha}-1)t^{\alpha,n}\frac{\partial\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g}}{\partial t^{\alpha,n}}=\sum_{\beta\in\operatorname{Eff}(X)}\left(\int_{\beta}\omega\right)\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g,\beta}+(3-\dim X)(g-1)\mathcal{F}^{X}_{g}.

(7.5)

If we introduce the perturbed first Chern class

\mathcal{E}(\mathbb{t}):=c_{1}(X)+\sum_{m,\sigma}(1-q_{\sigma}-m)t^{\sigma,m}\tau_{m}(T_{\sigma})-\sum_{m,\sigma}t^{\sigma,m}\tau_{m-1}(c_{1}(X)\cup T_{\sigma}),

and using the selection rule (
7.5
), the Hori’s constraint (
7.4
) (specialized to
g=0
and
\omega=c_{1}(X)
) can be reformulated as

\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathcal{E}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}=(3-\dim X)\mathcal{F}^{X}_{0}+\frac{1}{2}t^{\sigma,0}t^{\rho,0}\int_{X}c_{1}(X)\cup T_{\sigma}\cup T_{\rho}.

Taking the derivative w.r.t.
t^{\alpha,n}
,
t^{\beta,0}
we obtain

\displaystyle\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathcal{E},\tau_{n}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}-(n+q_{\alpha}+q_{\beta}-2)\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{n}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}-\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\tau_{n-1}(c_{1}(X)\cup T_{\alpha}),T_{\beta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}

\displaystyle\qquad{}=(3-\dim X)\mathopen{\hbox{${\langle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\langle}$}}\mathcal{\tau}_{n}T_{\alpha},T_{\beta}\mathclose{\hbox{${\rangle}$}\kern-1.94444pt\hbox{${\rangle}$}}_{0}+\delta_{n,0}\int_{X}c_{1}(X)\cup T_{\alpha}\cup T_{\beta}.

These recursion relations, restricted to the small phase space, are easily seen to be equivalent to (
7.3
). This proves (1), (4) and the convergence of
\Theta(z,t)
for
|z|
small enough, because of the regular feature of the singularity
z=0
. The proof of (2) can be found in
[
20
]
. Condition (3) follows from WDVV and string equation, as shown in
[
36
]
.
∎

In the case of Fano manifolds, we have the following analytic characterization of the fundamental solution
\big(\widetilde{\Theta}^{\alpha}_{\beta}\big)_{\alpha,\beta}
. Furthermore, because of Proposition
2.18
, we obtain another proof of (3) in the previous proposition.

Proposition 7.2
.

If
X
is a Fano manifold, among all fundamental matrix solutions of the system (
7.1
) for deformed flat coordinates,
25
25

25

Throughout the paper,
Y(z,t)=H(z,t)z^{\mu}z^{R}
has been denoted
Y(z,t)=\Phi(z,t)z^{\mu}z^{R}
.
there exists a unique solution such that, on the small quantum locus
(
i.e.,
t^{i}=0
for
i=0,r+1,\dots,N)
the function
z^{-\mu}H(z,t)z^{\mu}
is holomorphic at
z=0
, with series expansion

z^{-\mu}H(z,t)z^{\mu}={\rm e}^{t\cup}+zK_{1}(t)+z^{2}K_{2}(t)+\cdots,\qquad t^{i}=0\ \text{for}\ i=0,r+1,\dots,N,

This solution coincides with the solution
\big(\widetilde{\Theta}^{\alpha}_{\beta}(z,t)\big)_{\alpha,\beta}
.

Proof.

We already know from Proposition
2.18
that such a solution is unique. Let us now prove the main statement. In what follows, we will denote the degree
\deg T_{\alpha}
just by
|\alpha|
for brevity. By point
(2)
of Proposition
7.1
, we have that

z^{-\mu}\big(\Theta_{(z^{\mu}\phi)}\big)=z^{-\mu}\left({\rm e}^{z\delta}\cup z^{\mu}\phi+\sum_{\beta\neq 0}\sum_{\alpha=0}^{N}{\rm e}^{\int_{\beta}\delta}\bigg\langle\frac{z{\rm e}^{z\delta}\cup z^{\mu}\phi}{1-z\psi},T_{\alpha}\bigg\rangle^{X}_{0,2,\beta}T^{\alpha}\right),

with
\delta:=\sum\limits_{i=1}^{r}t^{i}T_{i}\in H^{2}(X;\mathbb{C})
. Specialising to
\phi=T_{\sigma}
, we have

z^{-\mu}\left(\Theta_{(z^{\mu}T_{\sigma})}\right)={\rm e}^{\delta}\cup T_{\sigma}+\sum_{\beta\neq 0}\sum_{\alpha,\lambda=0}^{N}\sum_{n,k=0}^{\infty}\frac{{\rm e}^{\int_{\beta}\delta}}{k!}z^{n+1+k+\mu_{\sigma}-\mu_{\lambda}}\big\langle\tau_{n}\big(\delta^{\cup k}\cup T_{\sigma}\big),T_{\alpha}\big\rangle^{X}_{0,2,\beta}\eta^{\alpha\lambda}T_{\lambda}.

In the second addend, we have non-zero terms only if

•

|\alpha|+|\lambda|=2\dim_{\mathbb{C}}X
,

•

2n+2k+|\sigma|+|\alpha|=\operatorname{vir\ dim}_{\mathbb{R}}X_{0,2,\beta}
.

By putting together these conditions, we obtain

n+1+k+\frac{1}{2}(|\sigma|-|\lambda|)=-\int_{\beta}\omega_{X}.

The assumption of being Fano is equivalent to the requirement that the functional
\beta\mapsto-\int_{\beta}\omega_{X}
is positive on the closure of the effective cone. This proves the proposition, the l.h.s. being exactly the exponents of
z
which appear in the above series expansion.
∎

Example 7.3
.

Notice that the solution (
6.21
) that we considered in the previous section for the computation of the monodromy data fo
QH^{\bullet}(\mathbb{G})
satisfies the condition

\displaystyle z^{-\mu}\big(\eta^{-1}S(0,z)\eta\big)z^{\mu}\qquad\text{is holomorphic near }z=0,

\displaystyle z^{-\mu}\big(\eta^{-1}S(0,z)\eta\big)z^{\mu}=\left(\begin{matrix}1-2z^{4}&2z^{4}&-z^{4}&-z^{4}&z^{4}&z^{8}\\
0&4z^{4}+1&-z^{4}&-z^{4}&0&z^{4}\\
0&0&1&0&-z^{4}&z^{4}\\
0&0&0&1&-z^{4}&z^{4}\\
0&0&0&0&1-4z^{4}&2z^{4}\\
0&0&0&0&0&2z^{4}+1\end{matrix}\right)+O\big(z^{9}\big).

This means that
\big(\eta^{-1}S(0,z)\eta\big)z^{\mu}z^{R}
coincides with the topological solution
\widetilde{\Theta}(0,z)
.

Appendix A
Proofs of Lemmata
6.2
and
6.4

In this Appendix we prove Lemmata
6.2
and
6.4
.
Before giving the proof of Lemma
6.2
, we recall the following well-known results (see, e.g.,
[
55
,
58
,
75
]
).

Theorem A.1

(Stirling)
.

The following estimate holds

\log\Gamma(s)=\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log s-s+\frac{1}{2}\log(2\pi)+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|}\right)

for
s\to\infty
and
|\arg s|<\pi
, and where
\log
stands for the principal branch of the complex logarithm.

Corollary A.2
.

For
|t|\to+\infty
we have

|\Gamma(\sigma+{\rm i}t)|=\sqrt{2\pi}|t|^{\sigma-\frac{1}{2}}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi}{2}|t|}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|t|}\right)\right),

uniformly on any strip of the complex plane
\sigma_{1}\leq\sigma\leq\sigma_{2}
.

Proof of Lemma
6.2
.

First of all let us prove that the functions
\varphi_{1}(w)
,
\varphi_{2}(w)
are well defined on the sectors

-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\frac{\pi}{2},\qquad-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\pi,

respectively.
We denote by
\mathcal{I}_{1}
and
\mathcal{I}_{2}
the integrands in
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
respectively, and
s=\kappa+{\rm i}t
. By Corollary
A.2
we have that

|\mathcal{I}_{1}|\sim(2\pi)^{2}|t|^{5\left(\kappa-\frac{1}{2}\right)}{\rm e}^{-\frac{5\pi}{2}|t|}|t|^{-\kappa}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi}{2}|t|}{\rm e}^{-\kappa\log 4}{\rm e}^{-4\kappa\log|w|+4t\arg w}.

The dominant part is

{\rm e}^{-\frac{5\pi}{2}|t|}{\rm e}^{\frac{\pi}{2}|t|}{\rm e}^{4t\arg w}.

In order to have
|\mathcal{I}_{1}|\to 0
for
t\to+\infty
we must impose

-\frac{5\pi}{2}+\frac{\pi}{2}+4\arg w<0,\qquad\text{i.e.,}\qquad\arg w<\frac{\pi}{2};

analogously, for
t\to-\infty
we have to impose

\frac{5\pi}{2}-\frac{\pi}{2}+4\arg w>0,\qquad\text{i.e.,}\qquad\arg w>-\frac{\pi}{2}.

Analogously, from Corollary
A.2
we deduce that

|\mathcal{I}_{2}|\sim(2\pi)^{3}|t|^{5(\kappa-\frac{1}{2})}{\rm e}^{-\frac{5\pi}{2}|t|}|{-}t|^{-\kappa}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi}{2}|-t|}{\rm e}^{-\pi t}{\rm e}^{-\kappa\log 4}{\rm e}^{-4\kappa\log|w|+4t\arg w},

and now the dominant part is

{\rm e}^{-\frac{5\pi}{2}|t|}{\rm e}^{-\frac{\pi}{2}|{-}t|}{\rm e}^{-\pi t}{\rm e}^{4t\arg w}.

In order to have
|\mathcal{I}_{2}|\to 0
for
t\to\pm\infty
, we find

\displaystyle-\frac{5\pi}{2}-\frac{\pi}{2}-\pi+4\arg w<0,\qquad\text{i.e.,}\qquad\arg w<\pi,

\displaystyle\frac{5\pi}{2}+\frac{\pi}{2}-\pi+4\arg w>0,\qquad\text{i.e.,}\qquad\arg w<-\frac{\pi}{2}.

Let us now prove that
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
are solutions of equation (
6.11
). We have that

\displaystyle\Theta^{5}\varphi_{1}(w)=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}-s^{5}\frac{\Gamma(s)^{5}}{\Gamma\big(s+\frac{1}{2}\big)}4^{-s}w^{-4s}\,{\rm d}s=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}-\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{\Gamma(s+1)^{5}}{\Gamma\big(s+\frac{3}{2}\big)}4^{-s}w^{-4s}\,{\rm d}s

because
z\Gamma(z)=\Gamma(z+1)
. Changing variable
t:=s+1
, and consequently shifting the line of integration
\Lambda_{1}
to
\Lambda_{1}+1
, we have

\displaystyle\Theta^{5}\varphi_{1}(w)

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}+1}-\left(t-\frac{1}{2}\right)\frac{\Gamma(t)^{5}}{\Gamma\big(t+\frac{1}{2}\big)}4^{-t}\cdot 4w^{-4(t-1)}\,{\rm d}t

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}+1}(-4t)\frac{\Gamma(t)^{5}}{\Gamma\big(t+\frac{1}{2}\big)}4^{-t}w^{-4(t-1)}\,{\rm d}t+\frac{2\cdot 4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}+1}\frac{\Gamma(t)^{5}}{\Gamma\big(t+\frac{1}{2}\big)}4^{-t}w^{-4(t-1)}\,{\rm d}t.

Note that in the region between
\Lambda_{1}
and
\Lambda_{1}+1
the two last integrands have no poles; so
\int_{\Lambda_{1}+1}=\int_{\Lambda_{1}}
by Cauchy theorem. This shows that

\Theta^{5}\varphi_{1}=4^{5}w^{4}\Theta\varphi_{1}+2\cdot 4^{5}w^{4}\varphi_{1}.

Analogously we have

\displaystyle\Theta^{5}\varphi_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}}-s^{5}\Gamma(s)^{5}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-s\right){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi s}4^{-s}w^{-4s}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}}-\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(s+1)^{5}\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2}-s\right){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi(s+1)}4^{-s}w^{-4s}\,{\rm d}s,

where the second identity follows from
z\Gamma(z)=\Gamma(z+1)
. Note that the integrand function is holomorphic at
s=-\frac{1}{2}
: indeed we have

\lim_{s\to{-\frac{1}{2}}}\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma\left(-\frac{1}{2}-s\right)=-1.

So in the strip of the complex plane
-1<\operatorname{Re}s<\frac{1}{2}
there are no poles, and by Cauchy theorem, we can change path of integration by shifting
\Lambda_{2}
to
\Lambda_{2}-1
:

\Theta^{5}\varphi_{2}=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}-1}-s^{5}\Gamma(s)^{5}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-s\right){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi s}4^{-s}w^{-4s}\,{\rm d}s.

Posing now
t=s+1
, we can rewrite

\displaystyle\Theta^{5}\varphi_{2}=\frac{4^{5}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}}\!\!-\left(t-\frac{1}{2}\right)\Gamma(t)^{5}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-t\right){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi t}4^{-(t-1)}w^{-4(t-1)}\,{\rm d}t=4^{5}w^{4}\Theta\varphi_{2}+2\cdot 4^{5}w^{4}\varphi_{2}.

This shows that effectively
\varphi_{1}
and
\varphi_{2}
are solutions.∎

Proof of Lemma
6.4
.

By Stirling’s formula we have that

\varphi_{1}(w)=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}{\rm e}^{\phi(s)}\,{\rm d}s,

where

\phi(s)=-5s+5\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log s+s+\frac{1}{2}-s\log\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)-s\log 4-4s\log w+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|}\right)

for
s\to\infty
and where
\log
stands for the principal branch of logarithm.
Let us find stationary points of
\phi(s)
for large values of
|s|,|w|
. The derivative
\phi^{\prime}
is

\phi^{\prime}(s)=-4+5\log s+\frac{10s-5}{2s}-\log\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)-\frac{s}{s+\frac{1}{2}}-\log 4-4\log w+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|}\right).

For
|s|
large enough, we have

\displaystyle\frac{10s-5}{2s}\sim 5-\frac{5}{2s},\qquad\frac{s}{s+\frac{1}{2}}\sim 1-\frac{1}{2s},

\displaystyle\log\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)=\log s+\log\left(1+\frac{1}{2s}\right)\sim\log s+\frac{1}{2s}.

Substituting these identities in
\phi^{\prime}
, we find that the critical point
\bar{s}(w)
in functions of
w
(for
|w|
large)

\displaystyle\bar{s}(w)=\sqrt{2}w+\frac{5}{8}+O\left(\frac{1}{|w|}\right).

Note that for
-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\frac{\pi}{2}
, the point
\bar{s}(w)
is in the half-plane
\operatorname{Re}s>0
, region in which there are no poles of the integrand functions in
\varphi_{1}
. So we can shift the line
\Lambda_{1}
in order that it passes through
\bar{s}
.
In this way we obtain

\varphi_{1}(w)=\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{2\pi{\rm i}}{\rm e}^{\phi(\bar{s})}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}{\rm e}^{\phi(s)-\phi(\bar{s})}\,{\rm d}s\sim\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{2\pi{\rm i}}{\rm e}^{\phi(\bar{s})}\int_{\Lambda_{1}}{\rm e}^{\frac{\phi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{s})}{2}(s-\bar{s})^{2}}\,{\rm d}s.

The computation of this Gaussian integral shows that

\varphi_{1}(w)\sim\frac{(2\pi)^{2}}{2\pi}{\rm e}^{\phi(\bar{s})}\frac{\sqrt{2\pi}}{\sqrt{\phi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{s})}}=(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{{\rm e}^{\phi(\bar{s})}}{\sqrt{\phi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{s})}},

where
\operatorname{Re}\sqrt{\phi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{s})}>0
.
An explicit series expansion shows that

\phi(\bar{s}(w))\sim-4\sqrt{2}w-\frac{5}{2}\log w-\frac{5}{8}\log 4+O\left(\frac{1}{|w|}\right),

whereas

\phi^{\prime\prime}(\bar{s}(w))\sim\frac{2\sqrt{2}}{w}+O\left(\frac{1}{|w|^{3}}\right)

and from this we deduce that

\varphi_{1}(w)\sim(2\pi)^{\frac{3}{2}}\frac{{\rm e}^{-4\sqrt{2}w}}{4w^{2}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)\right).

Let us now focus on
\varphi_{2}(w)
. From Theorem
A.1
we deduce that

\Gamma(-s)={\rm e}^{-\left(s+\frac{1}{2}\right)\log s}{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\pi s}{\rm e}^{s}\big({-}{\rm i}\sqrt{2\pi}\big)\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{|s|}\right)\right)

for
s\to\infty
and
s\notin\mathbb{R}_{+}
.
So,

\varphi_{2}(w)=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}}{\rm e}^{\phi(s)}\,{\rm d}s,

where

\phi(s)=5\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)\log s-5s-s\log\left(s-\frac{1}{2}\right)+s-\frac{1}{2}-s\log 4-4s\log w+O\left(\frac{1}{w}\right),

for
w\to\infty
. By computations analogous to those of the previous case, we find that
\phi
has a critical point at

\bar{s}(w)=\sqrt{2}w+\frac{5}{4\sqrt{2}}+O\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)

for large values of
|w|
. Note explicitly that for
-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\frac{\pi}{2}
this critical point is in the half-plane
\operatorname{Re}s>0
.

Figure 16:
Deformation of the path
\Lambda_{2}
.

By modifying the path of integration as in Fig.
16
, in order that it passes through the critical point, by Cauchy theorem we have

\varphi_{2}(w)=\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}{\rm e}^{\phi(s)}\,{\rm d}s-\sum_{p\in P}\underset{s=p}{\text{res}}\ \mathcal{I}_{2}(s),

where
P
stands for the set of poles in the region between
\Lambda_{2}
and
\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}
and

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}_{2}(s)=\Gamma(s)^{5}\Gamma\left(\frac{1}{2}-s\right){\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi s}4^{-s}w^{-4s}

is the integrand in
\varphi_{2}(w)
.
For the first summand we have an asymptotic behavior as before

\frac{(2\pi)^{3}}{2\pi{\rm i}}\int_{\Lambda_{2}^{\prime}}{\rm e}^{\phi(s)}\,{\rm d}s\sim\alpha\frac{{\rm e}^{-4\sqrt{2}w}}{w^{2}}

with
\alpha
a certain constant we will not need to specify. For the second summand, keeping into account that
\underset{z=n}{\text{res}}\ \Gamma(-z)=(-1)^{n+1}/n!
for
n\in\mathbb{N}
, and that
\Gamma\big(z+\frac{1}{2}\big)=\pi^{1/2}2^{-2z+1}\Gamma(2z)\Gamma(z)^{-1}
, we receive

\displaystyle\underset{s=n+\frac{1}{2}}{\text{res}}\ \mathcal{I}_{2}(s)

\displaystyle=\frac{(-1)^{n+1}}{n!}\Gamma\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)^{5}{\rm e}^{{\rm i}\pi\left(n+\frac{1}{2}\right)}4^{-n-\frac{1}{2}}w^{-4n-2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{n!}\left(\frac{(2n-1)!!}{2^{n}}\pi^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)^{5}4^{-n-\frac{1}{2}}w^{-4n-2}.

Therefore,

\sum_{p\in P}\underset{s=p}{\text{res}}\ \mathcal{I}_{2}(s)=-\frac{{\rm i}\pi^{\frac{5}{2}}}{2w^{2}}-\frac{{\rm i}\pi^{\frac{5}{2}}}{256w^{6}}+O\left(\frac{1}{w^{10}}\right).

In conclusion,

\varphi_{2}(w)\sim\frac{{\rm i}\pi^{\frac{5}{2}}}{2w^{2}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)\right),\qquad\text{for $-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\frac{\pi}{2}$}.

Let us now use the identity (
6.17
) in the following form:

\varphi_{2}(w)=2\pi\varphi_{1}\big(w{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\big)-\varphi_{2}\big(w{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\big),\qquad-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg\big(w{\rm e}^{-{\rm i}\frac{\pi}{2}}\big)<\frac{\pi}{2}.

It implies that

\displaystyle\varphi_{2}(w)\sim\frac{{\rm i}\pi^{\frac{5}{2}}}{2w^{2}}\left(1+O\left(\frac{1}{w}\right)\right)\qquad\text{on the whole sector }-\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg w<\pi.

∎

,

,

,

,

and

.

\displaystyle\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)_{0}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\

\end{matrix}\right),

\displaystyle\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)_{\text{\tiny$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{3}{4\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{3(2\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)}{2\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{\frac{6\gamma(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{19}{8}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{\frac{6\gamma(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}+\frac{13}{8}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{32\gamma^{3}+48{\rm i}\gamma^{2}\pi-6\gamma\pi^{2}+5{\rm i}\pi^{3}-2\zeta(3)}{2\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{-6\gamma^{2}+\frac{16\gamma^{4}}{\pi^{2}}+\frac{32{\rm i}\gamma^{3}}{\pi}+10{\rm i}\gamma\pi+\frac{7\pi^{2}}{48}-\frac{2(2\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)\zeta(3)}{\pi^{2}}}{\sqrt{c}}\end{matrix}\right),

\displaystyle\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)_{\text{\tiny$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{2\gamma+{\rm i}\pi}{2\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{11}{24}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{11}{24}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{(2\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)-2\zeta(3)}{6\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{768\gamma^{4}+1536{\rm i}\gamma^{3}\pi-1056\gamma^{2}\pi^{2}-288{\rm i}\gamma\pi^{3}+23\pi^{4}-96(2\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)\zeta(3)}{144\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\end{matrix}\right),

\displaystyle\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)_{\text{\tiny$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{2\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{4\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi}{2\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(2\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{29}{12}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(2\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{17}{12}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{(4\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+5{\rm i}\pi)-4\zeta(3)}{6\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{768\gamma^{4}+2304v\gamma^{3}\pi-2208\gamma^{2}\pi^{2}-720{\rm i}\gamma\pi^{3}+47\pi^{4}-48(4\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)\zeta(3)}{72\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\end{matrix}\right),

\displaystyle\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)_{\text{\tiny$\hbox{}\hskip 0.0pt\vbox{\vbox{\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt\vbox{\hrule height=0.3pt\hbox{\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt\hbox to5.4381pt{\hfil}\vrule height=4.35048pt,width=0.3pt,depth=1.0876pt}\hrule height=0.3pt}\vskip-0.3pt}}\hskip 0.0pt$}}=\left(\begin{matrix}\frac{1}{4\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{\gamma+{\rm i}\pi}{\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(\gamma+2{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{47}{24}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{\frac{2\gamma(\gamma+2{\rm i}\pi)}{\pi^{2}}-\frac{47}{24}}{\sqrt{c}}\\
\frac{(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+3{\rm i}\pi)(4\gamma+5{\rm i}\pi)-\zeta(3)}{3\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\\
\frac{768\gamma^{4}+3072{\rm i}\gamma^{3}\pi-4512\gamma^{2}\pi^{2}-2880{\rm i}\gamma\pi^{3}+671\pi^{4}-192(\gamma+{\rm i}\pi)\zeta(3)}{144\sqrt{c}\pi^{2}}\end{matrix}\right).

By application of the constraint

S=\big(C^{-}_{\rm Kap}\big)^{-1}{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}R}{\rm e}^{-\pi{\rm i}\mu}\eta^{-1}\big((C^{-}_{\rm Kap})^{\rm T}\big)^{-1},

we find

S_{\rm Kap}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&-4&6&10&-20&20\\
0&1&-4&-4&16&-20\\
0&0&1&0&-4&6\\
0&0&0&1&-4&10\\
0&0&0&0&1&-4\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{matrix}\right),\qquad S_{\rm Kap}^{-1}=\left(\begin{matrix}1&4&10&6&20&20\\
0&1&4&4&16&20\\
0&0&1&0&4&10\\
0&0&0&1&4&6\\
0&0&0&0&1&4\\
0&0&0&0&0&1\end{matrix}\right).

Now,
S_{\rm Kap}^{-1}
coincides with the Gram matrix
\mathbb{G}_{\rm Kap}=\big(\chi\big(\mathbb{S}^{\lambda}\mathcal{S}^{*},\mathbb{S}^{\mu}\mathcal{S}^{*}\big)\big)_{\lambda,\mu}
of the Kapranov exceptional collection.
